    every line you say is captured IS captured by a test that fails when the
    line is removed; retired tripwires; "recorded-absent → count divergence";
    silent carve skips), `serde-json-map-eq-ignores-order`, `sqlite-dqs-
    build-difference` (your two SQL statements quote no column DQS could
    mis-read: `SELECT <LOG_ENTRY_COLUMNS> FROM log_entries ORDER BY
    "createdAt" ASC` and `DELETE FROM log_entries`), `tier-r-vacuous-equal-
    rows` (a plant that fails both sides identically passes — your Log
    plants self-check their row count before the backup runs), `attribute-
    reds-by-stubbing-v4`.

## Mandate

Give v5 v4's two fixes at `236c04e35`'s shape, on top of `FILES`. (1)
**`cc5f2347c` — the Log rides a logical backup.** `db/log_entries.rs` gains
v4's two repository methods (`find_all_entries` — every entry oldest first,
`[]` while degraded or on a thrown query, DEBUG `All log entries read
{returned}`; `delete_all` — the whole table, `0` while degraded, INFO
`Deleted all log entries {deletedCount}`); the backup collects the Log after
the outfit history (DEBUG `Collected Log entries for backup {rowCount}`),
stages `data/log-entries.json` on EVERY archive (`[]` when empty, compact
included) and counts `manifest.counts.logEntries` LAST; the restore reads the
file OPTIONALLY, previews its count between `wardrobeHistory` and `warnings`,
restores it as step **22n-quater** (after 22n-ter, before 22o) — each row
re-created through the existing `LogEntriesRepository::create` with v4's
`CreateOptions` so `id` / `createdAt` / `updatedAt` are kept, the degraded
arm's WARN + warning, the per-row warning + WARN, the DEBUG on EVERY restore
— and reports `logEntries` in the summary; new-account mode remaps `id` /
`chatId` / `characterId` (a `null` character passes through); `clear_format3_
entities` gains the Log arm on the llm-logs connection so BOTH the `replace`
restore and "delete all my data" empty the table, which the per-user deletes
never reach. The THREE new `restore.ts` lines enter BOTH census whitelists;
the LANDED pins that said "the Log is not carried" are retired BY
MEASUREMENT (v4's staged tree and manifest dumped at the pin FIRST), each
named in the lane record; ONE DERIVED restore archive (never a rebuild)
exercises 22n-quater's three arms. (2) **`85c002822` — bugs 267 / 268.**
`TerminalManager::terminate(id, grace_ms)` ends a session the way closing a
terminal window does — SIGHUP, then SIGKILL after 3 s if `exited_at` is still
null, with v4's three `[PTY]` lines — and BOTH web routes use it; the async
`kick_all_for_chat` (no production caller, flushes Ariel first) is REPLACED
by the sync `dispose_chat_sessions(chat_id) -> usize` (terminate + drop each
of the chat's sessions, NO Ariel flush, INFO / DEBUG by count), reached from
the chat delete through contract **C5** (a defaulted method on
`TerminalLivenessProbe` returning `Result<usize, String>` so v4's `Failed to
terminate terminal sessions for deleted chat` WARN is LIVE; the `ChatDelete`
arm takes the probe — ONE `engine.rs` line); the orphan-transcript sweep
lands in core as daily-maintenance step **6b** over contract **C6**
(`TranscriptStore::list_transcripts()`; `FsTranscriptStore` implements it),
with the 1 h grace, the `is_live` probe, the `.log`-only filter, v4's six
lines, the NEW `orphanedTranscriptsSwept {scanned, deleted}` summary key and
the `orphan-transcripts` failure word; and — round ruling **R-O** — the
explicit `DELETE FROM terminal_sessions WHERE chatId = ?1` in `db/chats.rs::
delete`, v5's cascade on a generateDDL table that carries NO foreign key,
proven in `chat_delete_equivalence` against v4's FK cascade. (3)
**`c50cdddc6`** — measure and pin that v5's backup stages no
`terminal_sessions` file and counts none. (4) **H1 consumed** — the two
restore bridge sites switch to P4.D354's `_indexed` pair, so a restored
native-text upload is extracted, chunked and enqueued as v4's
`manager.ts:337` path does. Every unit arrives with its differential (the
six grown families — `system_backup_equivalence`, `system_restore_state` +
`system_restore_equivalence`, `backup_uuid_remap_equivalence`, `system_
delete_data_equivalence`, `maintenance_ops_tier2_equivalence`, `chat_delete_
equivalence` — over v4's REAL code; host unit tests mirroring v4's three
terminal test files where Node signals have no oracle), every log line
capture-pinned with its silence leg, every divergence a NAMED both-ways
table. You do NOT touch `services/mount_index/**`, `services/file_storage.
rs`, `tools/doc_edit/**` (P4.D354), `host.rs` beyond `FsTranscriptStore` +
the maintenance loop's probe (P4.D354's backfill call), `help/` / `docs/v4/`
(P4.D360 — HANDOFF H3), `apps/web/**` (no SPA half — the Ariel pane's Kill
button posts `?action=kill`, an unchanged wire), `fresh_schema.json` /
`migration_indexes.json` / `restore_vintage_state.rs`'s boot-align (UNMOVED —
neither commit carries a migration; a hunk there is a STOP). You land C5 /
C6 under their NAMES and consume C1 under ITS name; a departure from a name
is a STOP-and-flag.

  Log entry: ${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}\`);

## Tiered deliverables

### Tier 1 — must land

**The Log carrier (`cc5f2347c`)**

1. **`db/log_entries.rs` — the two repository methods.** `pub fn
   find_all_entries(&self) -> Vec<LogEntryRow>`: `SELECT <LOG_ENTRY_COLUMNS>
   FROM log_entries ORDER BY "createdAt" ASC` mapped through the existing row
   mapper, THEN DEBUG `All log entries read` `{returned}` (`target:
   "quilltap::db"`, the file's convention), through the FALLBACK helper
   `log_entries_read_or_empty` (v4's `withRawDb([])` — a thrown query is
   `[]` with the `Error reading all log entries` ERROR the helper's class
   emits; a degraded partition never reaches here — the CALLER holds no
   connection, the module doc's rule). `pub fn delete_all(&self) -> usize`:
   `DELETE FROM log_entries`, INFO `Deleted all log entries`
   `{deletedCount}`, through `log_entries_cleanup_or_zero` (v4's
   `withRawDb(0)` — `0` on a thrown statement with `Error deleting all log
   entries`). Red-first: a unit test in the file's `#[cfg(test)]` block
   plants three rows out of `createdAt` order, asserts the ascending read
   and the DEBUG's `returned=3`, then `delete_all` → `3` + the INFO, then
   `0` + the INFO at `deletedCount=0`; a table-less connection → `[]` / `0`
   with the ERROR class line (the silence leg: no DEBUG / INFO on the
   fallback arm). Mirror v4's `log-entries-repository.test.ts`'s three new
   `it`s by name in the test names.
2. **The backup collects, stages and counts the Log.** `collect.rs`:
   `BackupData` gains `pub log_entries: Vec<Value>` LAST (after
   `wardrobe_history :148`, with v4's `types.ts:436-441` why); inside
   `collect_user_data`, immediately after the `Collected outfit history for
   backup` DEBUG (`:791-794`) and BEFORE the `llm_logs` read (`:808`): `let
   log_entries = db.read_llm_logs(|conn| Ok(LogEntriesRepository::new(conn).
   find_all_entries())).unwrap_or_default()` serialised to `Value` rows in
   `LogEntryRow`'s key order (v4's `COLUMNS` order = `LogEntrySchema` order
   — `id, message, chatId, characterId, createdAt, updatedAt`), then DEBUG
   `Collected Log entries for backup` `{rowCount}` (`target: "quilltap::
   backup"`) — the degraded / absent partition answers `[]` through the same
   `unwrap_or_default()` idiom `llm_logs` uses (v4: "Empty while the logs DB
   is degraded"), and the DEBUG STILL fires with `rowCount=0`; the bag
   `:860-900` gains `log_entries` LAST (after `wardrobe_history :895`).
   `manifest.rs:104` → add `n(c, "wardrobeHistory", …); n(c, "logEntries",
   data.log_entries.len());` — the LAST count now (move the "LAST" comment).
   `staging.rs:142` → add `("log-entries.json", &data.log_entries),` after
   `wardrobe-history.json` with v4's `:718` comment ("The Log (4.11).
   Optional on the way back in, like the wear ledger.") — written on EVERY
   archive, compact included (`[]` when empty; it is not a derived embedding
   file, so `omit` never drops it). Red-first: the `collect.rs` unit module
   (`:1150-1460` — the wardrobe-history tests at `:1270-1345` are the model)
   gains a case planting two `log_entries` rows on an llm-logs connection →
   `data.log_entries` = the two rows in ascending order, the DEBUG at
   `rowCount=2`; an absent llm-logs connection → `[]` + `rowCount=0`; the
   manifest test asserts `counts.logEntries` is the LAST key; the staging
   test asserts `data/log-entries.json` = `[]` on an empty bag and on a
   `compact` backup.
3. **The restore reads, previews and reports the Log.** `restore/archive.rs:
   206` → add `let log_entries = opt("data/log-entries.json")?;` after
   `wardrobe_history` (v4 `:278`, the "optional, so an older archive just has
   none" why) and `log_entries,` LAST in the `BackupData` literal (`:253`).
   `restore/preview.rs:78` → add `log_entries: d.log_entries.len(),` between
   `wardrobe_history` and `embedding_reconcile` ("the 44th key, between
   `wardrobeHistory` and `warnings`" — v4 `preview.ts:75`). `restore/mod.rs:
   101` → `pub log_entries: usize,` after `wardrobe_history` (serde
   camelCase `logEntries`; doc: v4 `types.ts:637` — the rows written, COUNTED
   ROW BY ROW; the archive's count in the preview), before
   `embedding_reconcile`. The restore counters struct (`orchestrator.rs:
   4717`) and its projection (`:4838`) gain the field. Red-first: the
   `system_restore_equivalence` preview rows over the derived archive (item
   9) — `logEntries: 3` in v4's slot; an older archive (the base
   `restore-archive.zip`) → `logEntries: 0` on both sides.
4. **Step 22n-quater.** In `restore_on_writer`, immediately after 22n-ter's
   closing `}` (`:2133`) and before 22o's banner (`:2136`):
   ```rust
   // ── 22n-quater. The Log — P4.D355, v4 `cc5f2347c` `restore.ts:1231-1256`.
   //    "Lives in the LLM logs database, so it is skipped with the LLM logs
   //    when that database is degraded. Lines are written once and never
   //    edited, so each keeps its id and both timestamps." `data` is the
   //    REMAPPED bundle in new-account mode. Through the P4.D331 repository's
   //    `create` with v4's `CreateOptions` (id / createdAt / updatedAt kept);
   //    a refused row (the Zod shape — `message` empty or over 4000 code
   //    points, a non-UUID id / chatId / characterId) is ONE warning, never
   //    the step. The DEBUG fires on EVERY restore (`total: 0, restored: 0`
   //    included).
   ```
   `let rows = &data.log_entries; if !rows.is_empty() { match llm { None =>
   { WARN "Skipping Log restore — logs database is in degraded mode"
   {rowCount = rows.len()}; w.push("Log entries were not restored because
   the logs database is in degraded mode") } Some(llm) => { let repo =
   LogEntriesRepository::new(llm); for entry in rows { let opts =
   LogEntryCreateOptions { id: os(entry,"id"), created_at: os(entry,
   "createdAt"), updated_at: os(entry,"updatedAt") }; match repo.create(
   message, chat_id, character_id, Some(&opts)) { Ok(_) => c.log_entries +=
   1, Err(error) => { w.push(format!("Failed to restore Log entry:
   {error}")); WARN "Failed to restore Log entry" {entryId = <entry.id as
   text or "undefined">, error = %error} } } } } } } DEBUG "Restored Log
   entries" {total = rows.len(), restored = c.log_entries}` — all three
   lines `target: "quilltap::restore"`. **The degraded arm's gate:** v4
   checks `isLLMLogsDegraded()` only when rows exist; v5's `llm` is `None`
   for BOTH a degraded and an absent partition — phase 14 (`:1312-1331`)
   distinguishes them (`None if llm_logs_degraded || !data.llm_logs.is_
   empty()`), and for the Log the same shape collapses to `None` with rows
   present → the WARN + warning (rows absent → nothing, as v4). **The
   per-row field reads** come off the RAW archive `Value`: `message` as
   `s(entry, "message")` (a non-string / missing message → an empty string →
   `LogEntrySchema`'s `min(1)` refusal — v4 refuses the same row with the
   same ZodError text because `_create` validates the merged entity; pin
   ONE such row), `chatId` likewise, `characterId` as `os(entry,
   "characterId")` (`null` / absent → `None`). The warning's `{error}` is
   `DbError::Internal(<zod_error_message>)`'s Display — compare BYTES against
   v4's `error.message` on the derived archive's refused row (the
   `carrier-oracle-measurement-gotchas` note: Zod 4.6.7 `.max()` counts code
   points — the refused row uses an EMPTY message, not an over-long one, so
   the text is `Data validation failed: …` / the `too_small` issue on
   `message` — record the exact bytes both sides). Red-first: the three
   `system_restore_state` cases of item 9 fail before the step exists (no
   `log_entries` rows after a restore; the census lines absent).
5. **The new-account remap.** `uuid_remap.rs`: after the P4.D317 block
   (`:477-486`) add `// === P4.D355 (v4 cc5f2347c, uuid-remap.ts:760-764)
   ===` — per row the three fields `id`, `chatId`, `characterId` through the
   `UuidRemapper`'s per-id remap exactly as `remap_wardrobe_history_row`
   (`:1007-`) remaps its `id` / `chatId` / `characterId` / `actorCharacterId`
   — inside `each(&data.log_entries, |row| …)` (`:110`, the file's per-row
   map; a `Value::Null` `characterId` passes through; every other key kept
   in place and in order), collected as `log_entries`; the remapped bag (`:555-565`) gains `log_entries,` LAST
   (after `wardrobe_history :564`) — ALWAYS present (`[]` when none). Note
   the `id` IS remapped (a fresh id per line — v4's `remapFields` mints for
   `id` like every other entity's). Red-first: `backup_uuid_remap_
   equivalence` — the oracle's file table (`backup-uuid-remap.test.ts:127`)
   gains `['logEntries', 'log-entries.json']` as the 41st row ("ALWAYS
   present — `uuid-remap.ts:823`") and a `restorePath` case with three Log
   rows (one `characterId: null`, one naming a carried character, one naming
   a character the backup does NOT carry — the remapper's rule for an unknown
   id, whatever it is, must match: measure, do not assume) → `logEntries`

6. **`clear_format3_entities` gains the Log arm.** `services/delete_all.rs:

**The terminal half (`85c002822`)**

12. **`TerminalManager::terminate(&self, id: &str, grace_ms: u64)`** in
    `crates/quilltap-host/src/terminal/mod.rs` beside `kill` (`:708-727`),
    with `pub const TERMINATE_KILL_GRACE_MS: u64 = 3_000;` at the module's
    constants and v4's doc (`:629-635`) carried. Body: `let Some(shared) =
    self.get_session(id) else { DEBUG "[PTY] Terminate requested for unknown
    session" {sessionId}; return }`; `self.signal(&shared, SIGHUP)` — refactor
    `kill`'s body into a private `fn signal(&self, shared: &Arc<Session
    Shared>, sig: libc::c_int)` (unix: `libc::kill(pid, sig)` when `pid` is
    known, `0` → done; otherwise / on failure the portable-pty killer, as
    today) so `kill` = `signal(SIGTERM)` and `terminate` = `signal(SIGHUP)`
    then the escalation; the escalation is a DETACHED `std::thread` (v4's
    `setTimeout(...).unref()` — no runtime handle needed, callable from an
    axum handler AND from the writer-adjacent chat delete; R-3): `sleep(
    grace_ms)`; `if shared.meta.lock().exited_at.is_some() { return }`; WARN
    `[PTY] Session ignored SIGHUP; sending SIGKILL` `{sessionId, graceMs}`;
    `signal(&shared, SIGKILL)` — a failed SIGKILL (the killer's `Err`) →
    WARN `[PTY] Failed to send SIGKILL` `{sessionId, error}`. The thread
    holds the `Arc<SessionShared>` it captured, so a session DROPPED from the
    map by item 13 still escalates (v4's "Escalation still reaches a dropped
    session"). All three lines `target: "quilltap::terminal"`, structured
    keys exactly as named (never a `message =` field — memory note
    `served-host-heartbeat-and-message-field`). `pub fn terminate_default(
    &self, id)` is NOT added — callers pass `TERMINATE_KILL_GRACE_MS`
    explicitly (Rust has no default args; the two web routes and item 13
    pass the constant). Red-first host tests in `crates/quilltap-host/
    tests/terminal_pty.rs` (the file's real-PTY idiom), mirroring
    `pty-termination.test.ts` by name: (a) a `sh -c 'trap "" HUP; sleep 30'`
    session + `terminate(id, 300)` → alive after SIGHUP, `exited_at` set
    within ~1 s after the WARN `[PTY] Session ignored SIGHUP; sending SIGKILL
    graceMs=300` is captured (`global_capture` — the line fires on the
    escalation thread); (b) a plain `sh -c 'sleep 30'` + `terminate(id,
    1000)` → exits on SIGHUP, NO WARN after 1.5 s (the silence leg); (c) an
    unknown id → the DEBUG, no panic. Mark the three `#[cfg(unix)]`
    (Tier 3 records the other arm).

    Kicked all sessions for chat` v5 never had is recorded as v4-deleted):

    `self.terminate(&id, TERMINATE_KILL_GRACE_MS)` then `sessions.remove(

    RE-AIMED:** `terminal_pty.rs:448-465` → `dispose_chat_sessions_
    terminates_only_the_chats_sessions_and_drops_them` (two sessions of
    chat A, one of chat B → returns 2; A's gone from `list()` / `contains`,

### Tier 3 — explicit refusal arms and recorded NO-PORTs (never silent)

25. **The Windows / no-pid arm of `terminate`.** v4 on Windows: node-pty has
    no SIGHUP (`pty.kill('SIGHUP')` is a no-op or throws per platform). v5:
    `signal(SIGHUP)` falls through to the portable-pty killer where `pid` is
    unknown or `cfg(not(unix))` (as `kill` does today at `:725`) — i.e. a
    hard kill at once, no grace. Recorded in the function doc as
    `TERMINATE_NON_UNIX_IS_KILLER` (no refusal needed — the caller's
    contract "the session ends" holds); the host tests are `#[cfg(unix)]`.
26. **The job-child sentence.** v4's "In the job child the PTY map is
    empty" (`chats.repository.ts:351`) — NO-PORT (v5 has no forked child;
    the `CHILD_BUFFER_VISIBILITY` class of P4.D331). Recorded in item 15's
    doc comment.
27. **`Error sweeping orphaned terminal transcripts` (the safeQuery ERROR
    class) vs `Orphaned-transcript sweep failed — continuing`.** In v4 the
    sweep's `safeQuery` SWALLOWS a thrown listing (ERROR + `{0, 0}`), so
    `runSweep`'s WARN and the `orphan-transcripts` failure word are
    UNREACHABLE in v4; v5 follows THIS FILE's standing recorded divergence
    (`scheduled_maintenance.rs:36-49` — v5 records such failures in
    `failures`, "a strictly more informative shape on a path v4 …"): a
    non-ENOENT listing failure → the step-6b WARN + `orphan-transcripts`.
    Record the arm under the existing divergence's name (read `:36-49` for
    it; do not mint a second) with a unit test driving a `TranscriptStore`
    whose `list_transcripts` answers `Err("EACCES")`.
28. **The `.qtap` carriers** — the Log stays OUT of `.qtap` export / import
    (v4 at the pin; the ledger row `:594` says so) — item 24's regression
    line, NOT a port; a future catch-up ports it when v4 moves.

## Rulings made at planning (the planner's calls; the human may overrule before lane start — a STOP item if they do)

Items the HUMAN must rule on are marked ⚖ and listed first.

- ⚖ **THE NPM CACHE GAP** (round-wide; vendor survey §F.4 / §J.1): one
  online `npm ci` in a pin worktree BEFORE any lane starts; `npm ci
  --offline` at your pin must succeed or you STOP. Not this lane's to decide.
- ⚖ **R-1 — round ruling R-O stands: the explicit `terminal_sessions`
  DELETE in `db/chats.rs::delete`** (item 16). Alternative rejected: a boot
  ensure that REBUILDS `terminal_sessions` with v4's migration FK — a
  unilateral schema change on v5-provisioned instances (D23 forbids it; the
  P4.D205 `chat_informs` precedent chose the explicit delete). The human may
  overrule toward the FK; a reversal is a STOP for item 16 only.
- **R-2 — C5's disposer returns `Result<usize, String>`** (survey R-4) so
  v4's `Failed to terminate terminal sessions for deleted chat` WARN has a
  LIVE home (a stub can `Err`; the host's manager never does). Alternative
  rejected: `-> usize` (the WARN would be unreachable — a silent-arm class
  the review flags).
- **R-3 — the escalation timer is a DETACHED `std::thread`**, not a tokio
  task: `terminate` is called from axum handlers AND from the chat-delete
  path (C5), which may run where no runtime handle is guaranteed; v4's
  `setTimeout(...).unref()` is a detached timer too. The thread captures the
  `Arc<SessionShared>` (so a dropped session still escalates) and exits
  after at most `grace_ms` + one syscall. Alternative rejected:
  `tokio::spawn` through a stored `Handle` (works, but ties the host's
  terminal manager to a runtime it does not otherwise own).
- **R-4 — C5's hook lives in `delete_conversation_with_vault_sweep`**
  (v4's `ChatsRepository.delete(id, {syncVaults})` twin), not in
  `db/chats.rs::delete` (writer-thread, no probe in reach) and not in
  `api/chat_delete.rs` alone (v4 disposes on EVERY repository delete, not
  only the route's). The Brahma / help-chat deletes (`api/brahma.rs:471`,
  `api/help_chats.rs:646`) call `chats().delete` directly — Tier 2 item 21
  measures whether they can own a session before threading a probe.
- **R-5 — the orphan sweep is CORE code over the C6 seam**, the `.log`
  filter / id slice / `named` sets / grace arithmetic all in core (v4's loop
  verbatim), the host supplying only the directory listing with stats and
  the unlink. Alternative rejected: a host-side sweep (the summary key and
  the log lines would then be invisible to `maintenance_ops_tier2_
  equivalence`, which drives core's `run_scheduled_maintenance`).
- **R-6 — `unlink_transcript` widens to `Result<bool, String>`** (ENOENT →
  `Ok(false)`, removed → `Ok(true)`, other → `Err(text)`) so the sweep can
  tell v4's three outcomes apart (silent ENOENT vs the WARN with the error
  text); `cleanup_closed_sessions` (`:200`) keeps counting `Ok(true)` only.
  Alternative rejected: a second seam method (two ways to unlink).
- **R-7 — `run_scheduled_maintenance` gains `probe: Option<&dyn
  TerminalLivenessProbe>`** as its fifth parameter (the `is_live` v4 reads
  from `ptyManager.get`); the five callers are named in item 18. Alternative
  rejected: a global / `OnceLock` probe (invisible to the differential's
  `None` case and to the host's unwired assemblies).
- **R-8 — the Log read in `collect.rs` goes through `db.read_llm_logs(…).
  unwrap_or_default()`** exactly as `llm_logs` does (`:808-821`), NOT through
  a new strict reader: v4's `findAllEntries` is a FALLBACK read (memory note
  `strict-export-vs-v4-fallback-reads`); a degraded partition is `[]` and
  the DEBUG still fires at `rowCount=0`.
- **R-9 — 22n-quater's degraded gate is `match llm { None => … }` when rows
  exist**, collapsing phase 14's degraded-vs-absent distinction: v4 gates on
  `isLLMLogsDegraded()` only when `logEntryRows.length > 0`, and v4 never
  has an absent logs file; the one v5-only state (absent + rows present)
  takes the WARN arm — the more informative of the two, recorded in the
  step's comment.
- **R-10 — the refused-row vector is an EMPTY `message`**, not an over-long
  one: `LogEntrySchema.max(4000)` counts code points under Zod 4.6.7 and
  v5's `zod_log_entry_issues` must agree on the boundary — a boundary row is
  a GOOD second vector (Tier 2 if time allows: a 4001-code-point message of
  astral characters, both sides refusing with the same `too_big` text), but
  the Tier-1 proof must not hinge on the counting rule.
- **R-11 — ONE derived archive, four cases**, not four archives: the same
  rows exercise preserve / new-account / replace / degraded; the refused row
  rides every case (its warning is part of each case's expected bytes).
- **R-12 — the restore census grows +3 and ONLY +3**: the span adds exactly
  three `moduleLogger` lines to `restore.ts` (measured — item "What the span
  adds"); the count 83 → 86 (54 / 8 / 22 / 2). If the lane's re-grep finds a
  fourth, it is a sibling's — hand it off, never add it.
- **R-13 — C6's method keeps the contract NAME `list_transcripts` with a
  richer return type** (`Result<Option<Vec<TranscriptEntry>>, String>` — the
  missing-directory arm and the non-`.log` entries need it); flagged in the
  lane record as a TYPE departure under the SAME name so the planner's
  binding copy is corrected at unification, not silently diverged from.
- **R-14 — no `Cargo.lock` move, no new dependency** anywhere in this lane
  (`libc` is already the host's; `std::fs::File::set_modified` for the
  fixture mtimes; no `filetime`, no `nix`); a lockfile diff is a STOP.
- **R-15 — the `terminal_sessions` DELETE's WARN is v5's own line** (`[Chats]
  Failed to delete terminal sessions for chat`, the two neighbours' shape),
  recorded as NOT a v4 line (v4's FK has no failure arm) — no oracle capture,
  a unit test pins it; the alternative (silently `let _ =`) is the silent-arm
  class.

### 💸 for the dogfood pass (on a Friday COPY, after the round unifies)

- **The Log rides a backup** (no spend): a full backup of the copy carries
  `data/log-entries.json` with `manifest.counts.logEntries` = `SELECT
  count(*) FROM log_entries` on the copy's llm-logs file (measure BEFORE the
  backup), the DEBUG `Collected Log entries for backup {rowCount}`; a
  `replace` restore of that backup keeps every line under its id (`SELECT
  id, createdAt FROM log_entries ORDER BY createdAt` equal before / after)
  with `Deleted all log entries {deletedCount}` logged by the wipe and
  `Restored Log entries {total, restored}` equal; a `new-account` restore
  remaps `chatId` / `characterId` onto the new ids (one line's chat opens in
  the Salon and `log_read` shows it); a planted corrupt llm-logs sibling →
  the WARN `Skipping Log restore — logs database is in degraded mode` + the
  warning in the restore summary and nothing written; "Delete all my data"
  empties `log_entries` (count 0 after) while the main wipe runs as before.
- **Bug 268 live** (no spend): open an Ariel shell in a Salon chat, click
  Kill → the shell exits on SIGHUP at once (bash honours it; the row's
  `exitedAt` lands; no WARN); open another, run `trap "" HUP; sleep 9999`,
  click Kill → after 3 s the WARN `[PTY] Session ignored SIGHUP; sending
  SIGKILL {sessionId, graceMs=3000}` and the row exits; `DELETE
  /api/v1/terminals/{id}` on a third → the same ladder + the row gone.
- **Bug 267 + R-O live** (no spend): delete a chat with a LIVE Ariel
  session → `[PTY] Terminated sessions of deleted chat {chatId, count=1}`,
  the shell gone, the chat's `terminal_sessions` rows GONE on this
  v5-provisioned-or-not copy (`SELECT count(*) FROM terminal_sessions WHERE
  chatId = ?` = 0 — on the Friday copy v4's FK would also have done it; on a
  FRESH `setup` instance ONLY the explicit DELETE does — run the row on
  both); plant an old `<uuid>.log` (mtime 2 h ago) and a fresh one in
  `logs/terminals/`, then trigger the daily tick → `Swept orphaned terminal
  transcripts {scanned, deleted=1}` and the fresh one spared; the backup
  still stages no `terminal_sessions`.
- **H1 live** (no spend): restore a backup carrying a project-less `.md`
  upload → its `doc_mount_file_links.extractionStatus = 'converted'`,
  chunks present, an `EMBEDDING_GENERATE` job enqueued for the Uploads store
  (💸 only if the active embedding profile is REMOTE — set the Built-in
  TF-IDF profile FIRST); a restored PDF upload stays `'none'` (P4.D354's
  carve).

    → the v5 ensure (NEW `db/memory_keystone_repair.rs`) adds the column AND

    FLAG, not a tier (R-E) — `bucketCandidates` skips `memory.keystone`
    rows AFTER `rowsLoaded++`, BEFORE the `CONSOLIDATED` branch; `isDemotable
    = hot && AUTO && !keystone`; `policyProtectionOf` answers `'keystone'`
    FIRST and `sparedBy`'s key order becomes `{keystone, manual, digest,
    cold}`; a keystone fold survivor takes NO footnotes and an EMPTY patch
    but keeps links + the reinforcement delta; `cascadeEmptiedDigests` keeps
    a keystone digest whose last member retires (DEBUG `Keystone digest kept
    though its last member retires`) and its `added` now counts only rows
    actually doomed (`if (!grew) break`) — port as shipped; `protection()`
    answers `{score: 1, protected: true, protectedBySource: true}`. In
    recall, "every digest is cut before any keystone" holds only INSIDE the
    inner circle (`innerCircleTrimOrder` sorts `Number(b.keystone) −
    Number(a.keystone)` first): present-character keystones are LIFTED into
    the circle at `tier −1` ahead of that character's digests, self
    keystones join the anchors, and `renderArchive` / `renderHead` render a
    keystone's `content` first (`summary` first otherwise). `KEYSTONES_PER_
    SUBJECT_MAX = 5`; the v4 quirk — `POST /memories` with `keystone: true`
    answers 400 `The memory was saved, but not as a keystone: <msg>` AFTER
    writing the row — is ported as the bytes (Q3). `keystone` appears in
    EVERY parsed Memory (between `consolidatedAt` and `createdAt`), so every
    byte pin of a memory row moves; `hydrateRawRows` coerces number / bigint
    / null to a boolean and DROPS a string cell with the WARN.

    own commit; the unifier checks the handoff list. Ties go to the feature
    lane (the a11y edits are mechanical)." Every sibling SPA lane applies
    rules **R1–R12** (the binding text is Shared contract C9) to the files it
    owns and records each as a `HANDOFF:` for P4.D358's census; P4.D358's
    static census script takes an allowlist for sibling-owned files, which
    the unifier empties. Already CONVERGED (not reds): the sidebar switches,
    `Allow Any Character`, the edit box, the composer `Message`, the

    `role="dialog"`; `qt-modal` (77 hosts) carries `role` + `aria-modal` +

- **§R.5 — Log lines.** Every line this round ports or moves lands with a
  capture pin (the thread-scoped rig) AND its silence leg.
  - A line emitted INSIDE a `Db::write` closure runs on the writer thread and
    is INVISIBLE to the rig: run the home on the caller thread (memory note
    `capture-rig-writer-thread`) or use `global_capture`.
  - A `db::fallback` line renders the BARE SQLite / Zod message
    (`error_text`), never `sqlite error: …`.
  - A structured v4 field that is an array or object renders through the
    `…Json` file-layer convention; v4's camelCase keys are the keys; an
    `undefined` v4 field is OMITTED; child-logger bindings are fields on the
    line; `durationMs` and other wall-clock fields are MASKED.
  - Open before `captured_with`; a v4 catch UNREACHABLE through v4's real
    code is pinned NEGATIVE with a v5 fallback home.
  - **Migration lines follow the standing precedent** (P4.D249 / P4.D304 /
    P4.D315 / P4.D324, `host.rs`): v4's migration INFO / ERROR texts are
    NO-PORT and each boot ensure logs ONE v5-worded INFO per added column /
    table / index.

- **§R.7 — Red-first.**
  - **Measure red before touching source** — on unported `main` for the
    wave-0 lanes, on the `KEYSTONE` tip for its dependents (a contract
    refusal arm answering `not_available` IS the red). The lane record
    carries the red count per family. A test that passes before the fix is
    a test of nothing.
  - **Ruled divergences** are NAMED both-ways tables in the idiom of
    `FRESH_STORE_RESIDUAL` / `BOOT_CREATED_HISTORY_TABLE`, so a v4
    convergence trips them. This round's planned names: **P4.D345**
    `HOLD_KEEP_ALIVE_NO_STREAM` (v4's 15 s keep-alive ping — no v5
    counterpart by architecture), `ROUND_DROPS_MIN_EXCLUSIVE` (only if the
    corpus shows it); **P4.D354** `REFUSING_SEAM_LEAVES_PDF_UNEXTRACTED`
    (the backfill and the `_indexed` bridges leave a PDF / DOCX row at
    `'none'` while `DocumentTextExtractor::is_available()` is `false`; the
    REST path keeps v4's `failed` bytes); **P4.D347** `ECHO_SLICE_SURROGATE`
    + `SENTENCE_SLICE_SURROGATE` (a JS `slice` that cuts a surrogate pair —
    v5 rounds to the code-point boundary); **P4.D343** the dry scope's
    table-delta proof (whatever the `DryBuildContextSeams` wrapper does not
    cover is a recorded divergence, never a silent write) and `plugin_tool_
    effects` as NO-COUNTERPART; **P4.D350** `PUBLISH_AFTER_COMMIT` only if
    the human overrules its R-1 (v4 publishes at the write; v5 after the
    commit); **P4.D355** the explicit `terminal_sessions` DELETE on chat
    delete (v5's cascade stand-in on a generateDDL table with no FK — named
    in its record; R-R); **P4.D341** none owed (Q3's post-write 400 is
    ported as bytes); **P4.D352** an ADAPTER divergence recorded in the SPA
    (ProseMirror `Decoration.inline` for v4's CSS Custom Highlight API; the
    four `::highlight(name)` rules become class rules with the same tokens);
    **P4.D353** a Tier-3 code-block note only if R-B is overruled to (C).

- **§R.8 — The unifier's accumulation.** Each lane's record states its
  `Cargo.toml` versions at close; the unifier takes the HIGHEST and bumps +1
  for the union commit; `Cargo.lock` follows. The SPA: the highest of the
  SPA lanes, +1. CHANGELOG entries and `status-log.md` lane records
  union-merge (append-only).
- **§R.9 — One machine.**
  - **Workspace gates:** at most TWO Rust workspace gates at once. Count them
    with ONE `ps -ax -o pid,etime,command | grep "cargo test --workspace" |
    grep -v grep`; if two run, queue yours in ONE background command waiting
    on the siblings' PIDs (`while kill -0 <pidA> && kill -0 <pidB>; do sleep
    20; done; sleep $((RANDOM % 20)); <re-count once; if still two, wait on
    those PIDs>; cargo test …`), never on a pattern (memory notes
    `no-pattern-kill-on-shared-machine`, `parallel-lane-shared-resources`
    §2 — co-waiters all fire on one exit; re-count after the wait).
  - **Sweeps and Playwright:** one sweep at a time (announced); one
    Playwright run at a time (lanes by file only; the full suite is the
    unifier's). New own-instance e2e files' server ports are unique per
    lane (4319–4340, 4343–4350, 4352 and mocks 45301–45304, 45340–45348 are
    taken): **P4.D344 4353 / mock 45351**, **P4.D346 4354 / mock 45352**,
    **P4.D352 4355 / mock 45353**, **P4.D353 4356 / mock 45354**, **P4.D358
    4357 / mock 45355**, **P4.D359 4358 / mock 45356**, **P4.D355 4359 /
    mock 45357** (only if a terminal e2e is added). A lane that only edits
    an EXISTING spec takes no port.
  - **Long jobs:** `run_in_background: true`, full log in a lane-private
    file, awaited by the completion notification — NEVER a `pgrep`/`grep`
    poll loop (CLAUDE.md), never `tail -N` on a gate log. A gate reads its
    oracles and source at RUN time: no regen and no source edit while one
    runs (memory note `lane-gate-hygiene`).
  - **Targets:** clone main's `target/` before the first build (`cp -cR`;
    `/carryout` rule 8 — same volume only, memory note
    `apfs-clone-same-volume-only`); main's `target/` was warmed by the
    `/setupphase` chain on 2026-10-29 at the orders' commit (the test /
    clippy set the lanes clone; the release bins were swept — an
    own-instance e2e that prefers `target/release/*` must delete the stale
    release bins from its clone or build release, memory note
    `lane-spa-env-node-modules-and-release-bins`); a stacked lane may clone
    its parent lane's worktree target instead, when that lane is not
    mid-build.
  - **`apps/web/dist/`:** a fresh worktree has none — build it before any
    workspace clippy / test. **`apps/web/node_modules`:** `npm ci` in the
    lane (a cloned copy fails every vitest spec with NG0203).
  - **Concurrency:** the cap is the two-gate rule, not the lane count;
    twenty lanes do NOT all run at once (§S.0 waves). Disk at planning:
    144 GiB free on the repo volume with `target/` at 347 GB BEFORE the
    warm sweep — the human sizes the waves from the post-sweep numbers in
    the hand-off report.

  (2026-10-29): the npm-cache step (ONE online `npm ci` in a pin worktree

- **§R.14 — Stacking rules.** Each named stacking commit — `KEYSTONE`
  (P4.D341), `CONTEXT` (P4.D347), `SPINE` (P4.D348), `CATALOG` (P4.D350's
  FIRST commit), `FILES` (P4.D354) — is ONE commit whose subject starts with
  its name; the owner records the sha in its status header ON ITS LANE
  BRANCH the moment it lands. A dependent lane — P4.D347 / P4.D342 / P4.D351
  / P4.D356 from `KEYSTONE`; P4.D348 from `CONTEXT`; P4.D343 / P4.D345 from
  `SPINE` (which is on `CONTEXT` on `KEYSTONE`); P4.D349 from `CATALOG`;
  P4.D355 from `FILES` — finds the sha per `/carryout` rule 2 (`git log --all
  --format='%h %s' | grep -E '^[0-9a-f]+ <NAME>:'`, confirmed against the
  parent's lane-branch copy of the order) and branches from it — never from
  `main`, never a guessed sha; if the parent has not recorded it, STOP and
  report. A stacked lane may PRE-START from `main` on work that calls none
  of its parent's contract names — P4.D342's prompt bytes (the four
  extraction ladders + the consolidation tail) + the inner-circle resolver
  (`services/inner_circle.rs`, the tuning knobs); P4.D343's `tools/effects.rs`
  + `pascal/tool_effect.rs` + `StreamParams.tool_choice` + the `tool-build`
  re-aim (its unit E) + the pure `storyteller/*` modules; P4.D345's hold
  registry + eligibility / overrides / recompute / spans + the engine's
  `ReplayInputs` + the ONE corpus regen + the schema re-vendor; P4.D351's
  `services/annotations.rs` + `template_prompt_hint.rs` ports; P4.D356's
  NanoGPT half + the Tier R verify; P4.D349's `pronoun-presets` /
  `pronoun-guidance` ports + the image-prompt `humanoid` threading; P4.D355's
  `db/log_entries.rs` + the terminal host; P4.D348's `operator_post.rs` /
  `whisper_send.rs` / `turn_control.rs` additions and the funnel recorder;
  P4.D347 has no pre-start (its first unit widens `RawMessage`, which rides
  the keystone's row shapes) — rebasing onto the parent's commit before the
  first commit that does call a contract name; that rebase is the one
  sanctioned exception to "no rebase mid-lane". Two sibling-stacked parts
  (not lane-named commits): P4.D346's grammar-twin part (`pascal/custom-tool-
  types.ts` + the corpus copy + the spec header) stacks on P4.D345's Tier-1
  commit, or authors blind and lets the unifier run `custom-tool-types.
  corpus.spec.ts` — the order states which; P4.D359's chips stack on
  P4.D353's Tier-1 commit (the `message-content.ts` hook). Every other lane
  cuts from `main` at the orders' commit.

## §S — Unifier wires (what no single lane can do)

- **§S.0 — Waves (the recommended execution; the two-gate cap rules the
  machine, not the lane count).**
  - **Wave 0, from the orders' commit, at once:** P4.D341 (→ `KEYSTONE`,
    the Opus / Fable tier), P4.D350 (→ `CATALOG` as its FIRST commit, then
    its own units), P4.D354 (→ `FILES`), and in parallel P4.D360, P4.D357,
    P4.D358. That is six worktrees.
  - **Wave 1, as slots free (still from the orders' commit):** the five SPA
    feature lanes P4.D344, P4.D346, P4.D352, P4.D353, P4.D359 (each authors
    against the contracts with gated beats; P4.D346's grammar-twin part and
    P4.D359's chips wait for their sibling's Tier-1 commit).
  - **Wave A, from `KEYSTONE`:** P4.D347 (→ `CONTEXT`) FIRST, then P4.D342,
    P4.D351, P4.D356 (its NanoGPT + Tier R half may have PRE-STARTED from
    `main`). **From `CATALOG`:** P4.D349 — FIRST among the independents
    (R-H). **From `FILES`:** P4.D355.
  - **Wave B, from `CONTEXT`:** P4.D348 (→ `SPINE`).
  - **Wave C, from `SPINE`:** P4.D343 and P4.D345 (the two turn-side
    consumers every later union depends on).

  Within a wave every lane compiles against the C sections below it alone.
- **§S.1 — The pick order.** P4.D341 → P4.D350 → P4.D349 → P4.D347 →
  P4.D348 → P4.D343 → P4.D345 → P4.D342 → P4.D351 → P4.D356 → P4.D354 →
  P4.D355 → P4.D357 → P4.D344 → P4.D346 → P4.D352 → P4.D353 → P4.D359 →
  P4.D360 → P4.D358. The reasons per adjacency: `KEYSTONE` first (every
  server lane's row shapes, the dumps, the ensures); P4.D350 next because
  `CATALOG` is the base P4.D349 branches from, and P4.D349 right after it
  (R-H — the three LIVE-DATA erasure writers land before anything else
  touches a vault); P4.D347 → P4.D348 → P4.D343 → P4.D345 in their stacking
  order (`CONTEXT` on `KEYSTONE`, `SPINE` on `CONTEXT`, the two consumers of
  S1 / S2 / C1 / `TurnStopReach` on `SPINE`; P4.D343 before P4.D345 so
  `trigger_runner.rs`'s pre-run ceiling block precedes the post-run `Vetoed`
  arm and `realtime/types.rs` lands `storytellerRounds` NINTH before
  `customToolInterventions` TENTH); P4.D342 and P4.D351 (keystone-only
  consumers, no spine seam) after the spine chain; P4.D356 after P4.D345 so
  the `customToolIntervention` arm it writes for P4.D345 and the
  `punctuationWarnings` key it writes for P4.D352 are in the tree when their
  families re-run, and after P4.D351 so `settings-routes` is regenerated
  ONCE at the end of the server picks; P4.D354 → P4.D355 (`FILES` then the
  two bridge sites switched); P4.D357 before every SPA lane (the CSS lines
  the fenced appends sit beside); P4.D344 before P4.D346 (`_index.css`
  imports in v4's order; `RealtimeTopicAppend`'s SPA half NINTH then TENTH;
  `LibraryEntryKeys`' SPA badges in v4's order); P4.D352 before P4.D353
  (`renderMarkdownToHtml` is P4.D352's; P4.D353 post-passes its HTML);
  P4.D353 before P4.D359 (P4.D359's chips stack on P4.D353's `message-
  content.ts` hook; its `_variables.css` append sits after P4.D353's);
  P4.D360 LATE (its help / docs guards judge a union carrying every server
  feature; its schema-guard CHECK sees the final constants; the P4.D339
  precedent: fourteenth) — nineteenth here, before P4.D358; P4.D358 LAST
  (its census allowlist for sibling-owned HANDOFF files is emptied against
  the finished union; its toast-selector first commit is cherry-picked
  EARLIER by any sibling that edits one of the 20 spec files).

  §1 is restated at the close probe: the regen rule (PIN REQUIRED at the
  new baseline — v4 HEAD is already past it); the schema state (moves
  17–21 LANDED in ONE re-dump; `migration_indexes.json` +3); the LIVE-DATA
  block (vendor survey §I, sharpened by the keystone survey — **a v5 at
  `236c04e35` reads and writes:** `memories.keystone` (the 17th move, the
  migration's `INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` + the partial index `idx_memories_
  keystone` as ONE boot ensure), `chats.turnMode` / `storytellerInviteFilter`,
  `chat_messages.storytellerBid`, the NEW table `storyteller_rounds` (with
  its FK + both indexes as ensures), `llm_logs.storytellerRoundId` (the
  FIRST llm-logs column ensure v5 has needed — before the structural
  verify), `chat_settings.customToolIntervention` (default ON), `roleplay_
  templates.unmarkedStyle` / `markdownAsterisks` — and runs `778b97b2b`'s
  every-boot seed that re-encodes the built-in templates' `narrationDelimiters`
  as JSON and inserts the Novel template with it `null`. **The first v5
  boot of a pre-`8cb584eff` Friday copy ADDS the eight columns + one table
  (column-gated, v4's shapes, every ensure UNSTAMPED) and REWRITES the
  built-in templates' delimiter cells `*` → `"*"`; a copy v4 migrated sees
  NO-OP ensures and a no-op seed (measure: the seed must be idempotent
  against v4's own output).** **The THREE vault-key ERASURE hazards RETIRE**
  once P4.D349 lands: `render_physical_prompts_json` carries `humanoid` (a
  v4 `"humanoid": false` survives a v5 physical write and the P4.82
  backfill respects it), `PropertiesJson` carries `canDrawDiagrams` /
  `diagramStyle`, `build_wardrobe_item_file` carries `pronounGuidance`;
  until that lane is on the union build, **no v5 write to a character v4
  edited past `6588972d5` / `b27e5089a` / `eb90d9ed8`** (reads stay safe).
  **The Log is carried** (`cc5f2347c`, P4.D355): a v5 backup writes
  `data/log-entries.json`, a `replace` restore / delete-all clears
  `log_entries`, a `new-account` restore remaps `id` / `chatId` /
  `characterId`; `.qtap` still omits it; a pre-round v5 backup has no Log
  file — restoring it on the union leaves the Log empty (expected).
  **Keystones protected:** maintenance, demotion, consolidation, fold and
  chat-wipe skip `keystone = 1` rows; recall lifts them into the circle; a
  v5 backup / `.qtap` carries the column; a pre-round v5 build must NOT
  run maintenance on a copy v4 keystoned. **Terminals:** kill sends SIGHUP
  then SIGKILL after 3 s; a deleted chat terminates its shells (and v5
  deletes its `terminal_sessions` rows explicitly); the daily sweep unlinks
  orphaned transcripts older than 1 h (`85c002822`, P4.D355). **Settings:**
  the chat-settings PUT refuses unknown keys with a 400 (`3b8b6f42b`) — a
  v5 SPA caller that echoed a GET row back now fails (P4.D356's census).
  **Files:** ingest fills `doc_mount_blobs.extractedText*` for native-text
  uploads and a boot backfill fills old rows (`40fab1c54`, P4.D354 — a DATA
  WRITE on first boot of any copy with uploads: measure the row count; PDF
  / DOCX rows stay `'none'` under the refusing seam). **The general rule
  BITES: a v5 build from BEFORE this round must not open an instance this
  round's ensures have altered — the owed dogfood pass runs with the UNION
  build.** **NOT this round (`8832cfd20`, `bf7a457f6`):** v4 HEAD links
  SQLite3MC 2.4.0 / SQLite 3.53.4; v5 stays 2.3.5 / 3.53.2 — the first v5
  open of a post-`8832cfd20` Friday copy is the measurement; the iCloud
  placeholder scan divergence stands as recorded. **Standing:** no v5 build
  against live Friday; dogfood a COPY; do not round-trip v4-written
  characters through v5 writes while comparing with v4); the dependency
  facts (`openai` 7.45.0, `@openrouter/sdk` 1.5.9, `pdfjs-dist` 6.4.299,
  `pdf-parse` removed, `@quilltap/plugin-types` 2.9.0; the four client deps

  `help/` 137; `docs/v4` 518.

    `["[","]"]`, `None` → the text `null`); `pub fn decode_narration_
    delimiters(stored: Option<&str>) -> Option<StringOrPair>` (SQL NULL →
    `None`; a `[string,string]` → itself; non-string / empty → `'*'`;

    string); `marshal_rt_row` emits `narrationDelimiters` DECODED (JSON null
    when `None`), `unmarkedStyle` OMITTED on SQL NULL, `markdownAsterisks`

  - **C10 — the carriers:** the backup / restore / `.qtap` carriers for

    "<titleId>"` with `id="<titleId>"` on the visible title element (a
    per-instance id: `inject(UniqueId)` if the SPA has one, else a module
    counter `qt-dialog-<n>`); `tabindex="-1"`; the shared focus directive
    (`qtDialogFocus`, P4.D358's `ui/dialog-focus.ts`) for focus-in / Tab-trap
    / restore; Escape closes unless the host's busy gate says otherwise (keep
    v4's gate expression). For a `qt-modal` host: nothing — the primitive
    does it.
  - **R2 Non-modal floating surface** (`FloatingDialog`): `role="dialog"
    aria-modal="false" aria-labelledby`, `tabindex="-1"`, focus in on open
    and back to the opener on close, NO Tab trap, close button `aria-label=
    "Close"`, Escape inside closes it (topmost: `stopPropagation`), Escape
    elsewhere closes it only while `!document.querySelector('[aria-modal=
    "true"]')`, both skipping `defaultPrevented`.
  - **R3 Icon-only buttons** carry v4's `aria-label` byte: `Close`, `Cancel`,
    `Dismiss message`, `Close gallery`, `` Remove alias ${alias} ``, `Up one
    folder`, `Toggle model list` (+ `aria-expanded`).
  - **R4 Visible label ↔ control:** `<label for="<id>">` + `id="<id>"` on the
    control; never a bare `<span>` caption beside a control.
  - **R5 Heading-captioned control:** `aria-labelledby="<headingId>"` with
    `id` on the heading (ProfilesTab / ImageGenerationCard / ModelBehaviorCard
    / FieldSelectionStep / SuggestionCard / ContextCompression switch).
  - **R6 Placeholder-only / caption-less control:** `aria-label` with v4's
    string (never invent a string; where v4 has none, do not add one).
  - **R7 Switch:** `role="switch"` + `aria-checked` + a name (`aria-label` or
    `aria-labelledby`); the Prospero one is `type="button"` too.
  - **R8 Clickable non-button:** `role="button" tabindex="0" (keydown)=
    "activateOnKey($event)"` (P4.D358's `ui/activate-on-key.ts`) with
    `aria-expanded` (FileTable) / `aria-pressed` (gallery tile, quick-hide
    tags) where v4 sets them. Prefer a real `<button>` where the layout
    allows (v4 kept spans only inside `<tr>` / heading contexts and under
    the uppercase-button themes).
  - **R9 Live region for notices:** the toast container `role="status"
    aria-live="polite" data-toast-container`; an error toast `role="alert"`
    (P4.D358 only).
  - **R10 Colour-only state gets words:** `<span class="sr-only">Unmoderated
    chat.</span>`; ` (overheard)` appended to the whisper label.
  - **R11 Async result announced:** `<span class="sr-only" role="status"
    aria-live="polite">…</span>` (DocumentPane: `editingIndicator ??
    (saveLabel === 'Saved' ? 'Saved' : '')`).
  - **R12 Pointer targets ≥ 24 px** — CSS, P4.D357 (`.qt-tab-close`);
    templates: `min-w-6 min-h-6` on the inline copy-id button (P4.D358).
  The `e2e/support/toasts.ts` helper: `export const toastStack = (page:
  Page) => page.locator('[data-toast-container] > div')` and `toastByClass
  (page, 'qt-toast-error')` — P4.D358's FIRST commit with the 40 selector
  lines in 20 spec files + 5 in `ui/toast.spec.ts` + 3 source comments,
  cherry-pickable by any sibling that edits one of those files.
